<skill>
<name>investigate-conjectures</name>
<path>/etc/codex/skills/investigate-conjectures/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: investigate-conjectures
description: Develop, refine, and adversarially test open mathematical, computational, or mechanistic conjectures through bounded theory–experiment and multi-route proof-search programs. Use when Codex must formulate a non-vacuous research conjecture, recover or update a long-running research program, orchestrate an explicitly authorized search across independent proof approaches, separate proof obligations and claim levels, audit loopholes or competing explanations, design a mechanism-preserving discriminating experiment, interpret new evidence, or synthesize a supersession-aware research state. Do not use for teaching a technical source, solving an isolated mathematical problem, reviewing an external paper, or summarizing material that needs no research-state reconciliation.
---

# Investigate Conjectures

## Purpose

Turn a broad research question into a precise, falsifiable conjecture and an efficient program for resolving its weakest links. Integrate theory, adversarial reasoning, and only authorized computation. Preserve uncertainty: an honest open or inconclusive result is preferable to a stronger unsupported claim.

Treat the skill as the research-program controller. Use a specialized proof, teaching, paper-review, data, or coding skill for bounded subtasks when appropriate, while retaining the claim structure and evidence updates here.

## Load references selectively

- Read [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md) when starting, materially reformulating, or checking the non-vacuity of a conjecture.
- Read [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md) when resuming prior work, reconciling contradictory artifacts, updating conclusions, or writing a master report.
- Read [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) before declaring a loophole closed, a route impossible, a result decisive, or a theorem nearly established.
- Read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) only when computation is explicitly authorized or when the user asks for an experimental design. Do not run an experiment merely because it would be informative.
- Read [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md) when the user requests a bounded multi-route search for a complete mathematical proof. Use parallel agents only when the user explicitly authorizes delegation or parallel work; otherwise apply the same registry and round discipline sequentially.

## Establish scope and authority

1. Restate the research target in one sentence.
2. Determine whether the user authorized:
   - read-only synthesis or assessment;
   - new theoretical analysis;
   - experiment design only;
   - implementation or computation;
   - delegated or parallel proof search;
   - a bounded continuation under conditional branches.
3. Record any time, compute, token, data, model, or run budget as a hard constraint.
4. Ask only for a missing choice that would materially change the result. Otherwise make the narrowest reasonable assumption and label it.
5. Never expand a diagnostic or reporting request into implementation, computation, external actions, or a new research branch.

## Core workflow

### 1. Restore the authoritative state

Read the current primary artifacts before reasoning from memory. Identify the canonical statement, latest valid methods, raw evidence, unresolved objections, and superseded conclusions. Prefer derivations and raw results over summaries; prefer audited later results only when they actually repair or invalidate earlier work.

When resuming or synthesizing, maintain the ledger in [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md).

### 2. Freeze the research contract

Specify the canonical object, admissible inputs, observables, error criterion, topology or norm, horizon, limit order, approximation family, coefficient provenance, complexity dependence, and forbidden substitutions. State what information the proposed surrogate may retain and whether it must be autonomous and restartable.

Apply the non-vacuity checks in [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md). Reject oracle playback, hidden encodings of the target trajectory, replacement by an easier model, or quantifiers that avoid the intended compression problem.

### 3. Build the claim ladder

Separate at least:

1. exact identities or finite constructions;
2. well-posedness or internal correctness of the proposed approximation;
3. empirical accuracy on tested instances;
4. convergence of the approximation hierarchy;
5. identification with the intended limit or real system;
6. compact-horizon validity;
7. all-time or uniform validity;
8. existence of some admissible witness versus success of the current witness.

Do not promote evidence from one rung to another without an explicit bridge.

### 4. State the sharp conjecture

Write explicit quantifiers and dependencies. State:

- the admissible problem class;
- what may depend on accuracy, horizon, and regularity;
- what must remain independent of ambient scale or inaccessible trajectory information;
- the approximation guarantee and norm;
- coefficient provenance and initialization;
- autonomy, restartability, or finite-memory requirements;
- concrete falsifiers.

If several statements are needed, identify one central conjecture and label the rest as lemmas, assumptions, or stronger extensions.

### 5. Derive the exact causal skeleton

Derive before truncating. Mark each step as exact, conditional on named assumptions, heuristic, empirical, or open. Track causal dependencies, information flow, history dependence, and the source of every residual.

Keep distinct approximation axes distinct. Convergence in time step, basis degree, perturbation order, response depth, width, spatial resolution, or another coordinate does not imply convergence in any other axis.

In particular, separate:

- correctness of every fixed-order coefficient from convergence of the full expansion;
- production of omitted error from propagation or stability of that error;
- observable accuracy from state-space convergence;
- failure of a proof route from falsity of the conjecture.

### 6. Build a typed evidence ledger

For each central claim, record its exact statement, claim-ladder rung, status, assumptions, supporting and contrary evidence, dependencies, cheapest resolver, falsifier, and supersession relation.

Use calibrated labels. Distinguish proved, exact under assumptions, empirically supported, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded. Never translate “not observed” into “ruled out.”

### 7. Run a hostile audit

Attack the strongest plausible version of each alternative, not a straw man. Check for:

- hidden memory or inaccessible information;
- tail escape and high-to-low feedback;
- topology or observable mismatch;
- nonnormal amplification and unstable error propagation;
- unjustified interchange of limits;
- easier-model substitution;
- nuisance explanations matched only by weak controls;
- numerical conditioning, discretization, leakage, or selection effects;
- a result that validates one witness but not the intended existence claim.

Use [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) and state what each surviving objection would change.

### 8. Select one bottleneck

Rank candidate next steps by logical leverage, expected uncertainty reduction, mechanism relevance, and cost. Prefer the smallest step whose possible outcomes would change the research state.

Do not optimize for volume of algebra, number of experiments, or apparent completeness. Avoid polishing downstream machinery while an upstream identification or compactness gap remains decisive.

Normally pursue one bottleneck at a time. In an authorized multi-route proof search, retain a small structurally diverse portfolio and select one explicit bottleneck per active route using [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md).

### 9. Precommit any authorized experiment

Before implementation or execution, read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) and freeze:

- competing hypotheses;
- the disputed mechanism and why the testbed preserves it;
- primary metric and matched controls;
- pass, fail, and inconclusive thresholds;
- numerical validity gates;
- replication rule and conditional branches;
- exact budget and terminal stopping condition.

Use the smallest nondegenerate testbed, not automatically the smallest instance. Do not launch exploratory grids when one theory-selected stress or ablation can discriminate the hypotheses.

### 10. Update causally

After a derivation or experiment, ask:

1. What mechanism was supported, rejected, or left unresolved?
2. Which exact ledger claims change status?
3. Does a failure kill the broad conjecture, the current witness, one assumption, or only one proof route?
4. Which earlier conclusion is superseded?
5. Does the result authorize a precommitted branch? If not, stop.

Salvage valid identities, tools, counterexamples, and mechanisms from failed programs. Do not preserve their invalid conclusion.

### 11. Synthesize the current state

Lead with the strongest defensible conclusion. Report separately:

- established results;
- supported but unproved claims;
- disfavored or falsified explanations;
- unresolved decisive gaps;
- superseded conclusions;
- the single highest-leverage next action, if requested and authorized.

When later work corrects earlier work, use the corrected result in the main narrative and record the supersession once. Do not accumulate mutually inconsistent report layers.

## Decision rules

- **Strong surrogate performance:** Treat it as evidence of utility, not hierarchy convergence or arbitrary-accuracy existence.
- **A successful matched nonlinear or causal control:** Downgrade that particular mundane explanation; do not infer a theorem.
- **A failed high-order trend:** Check parity, conditioning, common-reference comparability, and replication before inferring divergence.
- **A correct local expansion with growing coefficients:** Preserve the local algebra while rejecting unsupported positive-horizon convergence.
- **A stability theorem without a source estimate:** Record propagation control; leave error production open.
- **Failure of one representation:** Distinguish failure of the explicit witness from failure of the broader admissible class.
- **Confounded evidence:** Mark inconclusive and stop unless a resolution run was preauthorized.
- **Budget exhausted:** Stop even when the scientific question remains open.

## Output standards

Match the requested artifact. For a concise assessment, provide the current conclusion, decisive evidence, and leading gap. For a full research state, provide:

1. canonical target and research contract;
2. sharp conjecture;
3. exact causal skeleton;
4. claim and evidence ledger;
5. adversarial alternatives;
6. experimental evidence and validity limits;
7. what is established, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded;
8. ranked proof obligations or next decision.

Keep notation stable and minimal. Attach claims to evidence close to where they appear. State assumptions at the claim they support. Never hide a decisive gap in a generic limitations section.

</skill>

---
name: solve-math-rigorously
description: Produce compact but complete solutions to standalone mathematical questions and proof requests. Use when the user asks to solve, prove, derive, analyze, verify, or correct a mathematical problem that is not primarily an incremental lesson about supplied technical documents. Infer the strongest reasonable interpretation without inventing assumptions, establish a minimal setup, use minimal notation, expose every nontrivial step, state and verify external theorem hypotheses, give proof architecture when useful, and silently audit the draft before answering.
---

# Solve Mathematics Rigorously

Produce a complete mathematical answer rather than a staged document lesson, unless the user explicitly requests incremental pacing. Optimize simultaneously for correctness, rigor, clarity, and economy.

## Determine the intended problem

Interpret the strongest reasonable version supported by the wording and mathematical context. Do not exploit an omission to answer a trivial or uninteresting straw-man version.

Handle ambiguity according to its effect:

- If it does not change the substance, adopt the conventional interpretation and state it briefly.
- If one reasonable assumption makes the intended problem precise, state that assumption before solving.
- If several reasonable interpretations lead to materially different answers, ask for clarification or give a compact case split when that fully resolves the ambiguity.
- If a missing assumption is necessary for the claimed conclusion, do not invent it silently.

If the stated claim is false, say so and give a decisive counterexample or contradiction. Present a corrected nearby statement only when it helps, and label it as a correction rather than the original result.

Answer the problem actually asked. Do not weaken a universal claim to a convenient special case, replace an exact question with a numerical approximation, or prove a converse instead of the stated direction.

## Establish a minimal setup

Begin with a compact setup or preliminaries passage when it reduces ambiguity or supports later reasoning. State only the necessary:

- domain and assumptions;
- definitions used in the solution;
- boundary, regularity, integrability, independence, or nondegeneracy conditions that matter;
- notation that must persist through the answer.

Reuse the problem's notation whenever possible. Introduce a new symbol only when the unabbreviated expression is genuinely cumbersome and recurs often. Define every new symbol before use and state its mathematical type when that is not immediately evident.

Do not force a visible setup heading for a short elementary problem. Do not hide substantive assumptions in prose later in the solution.

## Give the architecture before a substantial proof

Before a long or conceptually difficult proof, explain the plan in a short paragraph or compact list. Identify the main steps, cases, claims, or lemmas and how they combine to yield the conclusion.

Keep the architecture at the level needed to orient the reader. Do not duplicate the detailed proof, and do not substitute a plan for the proof itself. For a routine calculation or one-step argument, omit the architecture.

## Execute every nontrivial step

Derive each nontrivial algebraic, analytic, probabilistic, geometric, combinatorial, or logical transition. Give enough detail for the user to verify the step without reconstructing missing reasoning.

In particular:

- identify the definition, identity, inequality, or prior claim being used;
- show substitutions, rearrangements, case transitions, and changes of variables that carry real mathematical content;
- track domains, signs, quantifiers, equality conditions, exceptional cases, and possible zero denominators;
- justify limit exchanges, differentiation or integration under a sign, convergence claims, and optimization steps;
- distinguish exact arguments from heuristics, approximations, and numerical evidence;
- show that intermediate claims actually imply the requested conclusion.

Avoid words such as “clearly,” “obviously,” “standard,” and “it follows” when they replace reasoning. They are acceptable only after the supporting reason has been supplied.

Do not over-explain elementary arithmetic or definitions already established. Compress routine steps while preserving the chain of verification.

## Invoke external results completely

Whenever the solution relies on a nontrivial theorem or external fact:

1. state the exact form needed, including its assumptions and conclusion;
2. verify each assumption in the present problem;
3. identify the current objects substituted into the theorem;
4. state exactly what conclusion the theorem yields here.

Do not merely name a theorem or call a fact standard. Prefer the relevant specialization over an unnecessarily general statement.

Verify a recent, specialized, source-specific, or uncertain result against a primary or authoritative source before relying on it. Cite the source near the claim. Paraphrase the relevant result unless its exact wording matters; quote only a short compliant excerpt when useful.

If the required theorem is unavailable, uncertain, or does not apply, expose the gap instead of proceeding as though it were valid.

## Use examples and checks proportionately

Include an example, boundary case, counterexample, or remark only when it clarifies an abstraction, tests a hypothesis, explains a consequence, or exposes why an assumption is needed.

Use low-cost sanity checks when they add confidence, such as:

- substituting the result back into the defining relation;
- checking dimensions, signs, or units;
- testing a boundary or small case;
- comparing with a known special case;
- deriving the same conclusion by a second short route.

Do not add examples mechanically or let them replace the general argument.

## Keep the presentation compact

Use headings only when they make a substantial solution easier to navigate. Keep notation sparse and stable. Prefer explicit expressions to disposable shorthand.

Use equations to carry mathematical content, not decoration. Avoid formula dumps, decorative summaries, and boxed equations. State the conclusion plainly.

Let the problem's complexity determine the answer length. Preserve every necessary assumption, theorem check, and logical transition, but remove repetition and exposition that does not improve understanding or verification.

## Audit and revise silently

Draft the solution, then review it before sending. Check:

1. Did the solution address the strongest reasonable interpretation rather than a straw man?
2. Were all adopted assumptions stated, and were none invented merely to force the result?
3. Was the setup sufficient but minimal?
4. Was every persistent symbol necessary and defined?
5. Did a substantial proof receive a useful architecture?
6. Can the reader verify every nontrivial transition?
7. Was every external theorem stated in the needed form, with its hypotheses checked and its conclusion applied explicitly?
8. Were recent, specialized, or uncertain results verified from an authoritative source?
9. Were edge cases, equality conditions, domains, and quantifiers handled?
10. Does the final conclusion answer the exact question?
11. Do examples or remarks earn their space?
12. Is the answer as compact as rigor allows, with no boxed equations?

Revise any failing part and repeat the audit until the answer satisfies the checklist as well as possible. Do not mention the drafting or audit process in the final answer.

</skill>

Yes. The \(L=3\) result—and the general finite-\((L,t)\) theorem—are now proved with the width-first limit order.

Let

\[
D_{t,L}(\eta)=F_{t,L}(2\eta)-F_{2t,L}(\eta).
\]

For \(\phi\in C^{12}\) with the stated weighted derivative envelope and RMS normalization,

\[
\boxed{
\left|
D_{t,L}(\eta)
+\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,L}\eta^3
\right|
\le
B_\phi^{E_{L,2t}}|\eta|^5,
\qquad |\eta|\le\frac12,
}
\]

where

\[
B_\phi=\max\{4,M_\phi\}.
\]

The activation number \(J_{\phi,L}\) is given explicitly by nine one-dimensional Gaussian moments and a terminating forward/reverse depth recursion. In particular,

\[
\kappa_{\phi,L,t}
=-\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,L},
\]

so the leading discrepancy is exactly quadratic in \(t\).

All depth/time dependence in the remainder is numerical. For \(L\ge2\),

\[
M_{L,2t}=(4t+1)(L-1),
\]

\[
E_{L,2t}
=
2L\,p_{2t}^{M_{L,2t}}
+r_{2t}
\frac{p_{2t}^{M_{L,2t}}-1}{p_{2t}-1},
\]

with \(p_N,r_N\) defined by the explicit terminating integer recursion in the proof.

For \(L=3\),

\[
E_{3,2t}
=
6p_{2t}^{8t+2}
+r_{2t}\frac{p_{2t}^{8t+2}-1}{p_{2t}-1},
\]

and

\[
J_{\phi,3}
=
T_3+3M_3+
4\!\left(
V_3+\beta_1+\beta_2+d^2V_1+\beta_3+dV_2
\right).
\]

Therefore,

\[
\boxed{
\left|
F_{t,3}(2\eta)-F_{2t,3}(\eta)
+\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,3}\eta^3
\right|
\le B_\phi^{E_{3,2t}}|\eta|^5.
}
\]

The requested concrete case is

\[
\boxed{
\left|
F_{2,3}(2\eta)-F_{4,3}(\eta)
+3J_{\phi,3}\eta^3
\right|
\le B_\phi^{E_{3,4}}|\eta|^5.
}
\]

Consequently,

\[
|D_{t,L}(\eta)|
\le
\left(
\frac{t(2t-1)}2|J_{\phi,L}|+\varepsilon
\right)|\eta|^3
\]

whenever

\[
|\eta|
\le
\min\left\{
\frac12,\,
\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+B_\phi^{E_{L,2t}}}}
\right\}.
\]

The previous intertwining gap was closed using fixed bounded Gaussian operators

\[
W_{a,0}=I_a+J_a^*,
\]

whose adjoints generate the complete response terms even for singular histories and nonlinear moving queries. This construction received two independent hostile PASS verdicts.

The complete proof is in [PROOF.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/PROOF.md). Supporting proofs and audits:

- [Cubic operator bridge](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/CUBIC_DEPTH_TIME.md)
- [Fixed-step width limit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/WIDTH_DEPTH_TIME.md)

- domain and assumptions;
- definitions used in the solution;
- boundary, regularity, integrability, independence, or nondegeneracy conditions that matter;
- notation that must persist through the answer.

Reuse the problem's notation whenever possible. Introduce a new symbol only when the unabbreviated expression is genuinely cumbersome and recurs often. Define every new symbol before use and state its mathematical type when that is not immediately evident.

Do not force a visible setup heading for a short elementary problem. Do not hide substantive assumptions in prose later in the solution.

## Give the architecture before a substantial proof

Before a long or conceptually difficult proof, explain the plan in a short paragraph or compact list. Identify the main steps, cases, claims, or lemmas and how they combine to yield the conclusion.

Keep the architecture at the level needed to orient the reader. Do not duplicate the detailed proof, and do not substitute a plan for the proof itself. For a routine calculation or one-step argument, omit the architecture.

## Execute every nontrivial step

Derive each nontrivial algebraic, analytic, probabilistic, geometric, combinatorial, or logical transition. Give enough detail for the user to verify the step without reconstructing missing reasoning.

In particular:

- identify the definition, identity, inequality, or prior claim being used;
- show substitutions, rearrangements, case transitions, and changes of variables that carry real mathematical content;
- track domains, signs, quantifiers, equality conditions, exceptional cases, and possible zero denominators;
- justify limit exchanges, differentiation or integration under a sign, convergence claims, and optimization steps;
- distinguish exact arguments from heuristics, approximations, and numerical evidence;
- show that intermediate claims actually imply the requested conclusion.

Avoid words such as “clearly,” “obviously,” “standard,” and “it follows” when they replace reasoning. They are acceptable only after the supporting reason has been supplied.

Do not over-explain elementary arithmetic or definitions already established. Compress routine steps while preserving the chain of verification.

## Invoke external results completely

Whenever the solution relies on a nontrivial theorem or external fact:

1. state the exact form needed, including its assumptions and conclusion;
2. verify each assumption in the present problem;
3. identify the current objects substituted into the theorem;
4. state exactly what conclusion the theorem yields here.

Do not merely name a theorem or call a fact standard. Prefer the relevant specialization over an unnecessarily general statement.

Verify a recent, specialized, source-specific, or uncertain result against a primary or authoritative source before relying on it. Cite the source near the claim. Paraphrase the relevant result unless its exact wording matters; quote only a short compliant excerpt when useful.

If the required theorem is unavailable, uncertain, or does not apply, expose the gap instead of proceeding as though it were valid.

## Use examples and checks proportionately

Include an example, boundary case, counterexample, or remark only when it clarifies an abstraction, tests a hypothesis, explains a consequence, or exposes why an assumption is needed.

Use low-cost sanity checks when they add confidence, such as:

- substituting the result back into the defining relation;
- checking dimensions, signs, or units;
- testing a boundary or small case;
- comparing with a known special case;
- deriving the same conclusion by a second short route.

Do not add examples mechanically or let them replace the general argument.

## Keep the presentation compact

Use headings only when they make a substantial solution easier to navigate. Keep notation sparse and stable. Prefer explicit expressions to disposable shorthand.

Use equations to carry mathematical content, not decoration. Avoid formula dumps, decorative summaries, and boxed equations. State the conclusion plainly.

Let the problem's complexity determine the answer length. Preserve every necessary assumption, theorem check, and logical transition, but remove repetition and exposition that does not improve understanding or verification.

## Audit and revise silently

Draft the solution, then review it before sending. Check:

1. Did the solution address the strongest reasonable interpretation rather than a straw man?
2. Were all adopted assumptions stated, and were none invented merely to force the result?
3. Was the setup sufficient but minimal?
4. Was every persistent symbol necessary and defined?
5. Did a substantial proof receive a useful architecture?
6. Can the reader verify every nontrivial transition?
7. Was every external theorem stated in the needed form, with its hypotheses checked and its conclusion applied explicitly?
8. Were recent, specialized, or uncertain results verified from an authoritative source?
9. Were edge cases, equality conditions, domains, and quantifiers handled?
10. Does the final conclusion answer the exact question?
11. Do examples or remarks earn their space?
12. Is the answer as compact as rigor allows, with no boxed equations?

Revise any failing part and repeat the audit until the answer satisfies the checklist as well as possible. Do not mention the drafting or audit process in the final answer.

</skill>

The requested actual-network theorem is still open under the stated \(C^{12}\), bounded-derivative, at-most-linear-growth assumptions. I cannot rigorously claim

\[
\left|
F_{t,L}(2\eta)-F_{2t,L}(\eta)
+\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,L}\eta^3
\right|
\le C_{\phi,L}t^4|\eta|^5
\]

with activation-defined \(C_{\phi,L}\) on \(|\eta|\le c_{\phi,L}/t\).

What is now proved rigorously is the sharp \(t^4\) reduction. Writing

\[
E_h=I+hg,\qquad C_h=E_{2h},\qquad P_h=E_h^2,
\]

the local defect factors exactly:

\[
P_hx-C_hx
=
h^2\int_0^1Dg(x+shg(x))[g(x)]\,ds.
\]

Hybrid telescoping consequently gives

\[
D_{t,L}(h)=h^2Q_{t,L}(h).
\]

Since \(D_{t,L}\) is odd, \(Q_{t,L}\) is odd, and therefore

\[
\left|D_{t,L}(h)-\kappa_{\phi,L,t}h^3\right|
\le
\frac{|h|^5}{6}
\sup_{|u|\le |h|}
|Q_{t,L}'''(u)|.
\]

For any genuine \(C^4\) Banach-space vector field and observable whose derivatives are bounded by \(K\), an explicit jet induction proves

\[
\sup_{|u|\le c_K/t}|Q_t'''(u)|
\le 6C_Kt^4,
\]

with the valid coarse choices

\[
c_K=\frac1{64K^2},
\qquad
C_K=10^9K^{10}.
\]

Thus the desired inequality follows conditionally if one proves the network-specific estimate

\[
\sup_{\substack{t\ge1,\;0\le j<t\\ |h|t\le c_{\phi,L}}}
t^{-3}|A_{j,t}'''(h)|
\le K_{\phi,L},
\]

for the transported local defects \(A_{j,t}\), with \(K_{\phi,L}\) constructed solely from \(M_\phi\), Gaussian moments, and \(L\). This is the unresolved \(\mathrm{UGC}_4(\phi,L)\) lemma.

The gap is genuine:

- The existing operator bridge proves generated-core regularity only for each fixed finite horizon, without a horizon-uniform constant.
- The fixed-\(t\) Price compiler gives \(B_\phi^{E_{L,2t}}\), not a \(t^4\) estimate.
- \(W=I+J^*\) is only \(L^2\)-bounded; it has no ambient \(L^q\to L^p\) bound sufficient for the required products.
- For admissible non-affine activations, the Nemytskii map \(u\mapsto\phi(u)\) need not even be \(C^2:L^2\to L^2\).

The exponent \(t^4\) is nevertheless sharp. For \(L=1\) and \(\phi(x)=x\),

\[
F_{N,1}(h)
=
\frac{(1+h)^{2N}-(1-h)^{2N}}2,
\]

and the fifth-order coefficient of \(F_{t,1}(2h)-F_{2t,1}(h)\) is

\[
32{2t\choose5}-{4t\choose5}
=
-\frac43t(t-1)(2t-1)(8t-9),
\]

which has degree four.

Both independent proof routes and the hostile audit reached the same verdict:

- [Uniform reduction proof](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/UNIFORM_BSERIES.md)

- [Adversarial audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/AUDIT_UNIFORM_BSERIES.md)

So the quoted statement remains correct: the \(t^4\) mechanism is proved and sharp, but the unconditional reused-matrix MLP bound remains open precisely at the horizon-uniform generated-response estimate.

<skill>
<name>solve-math-rigorously</name>
<path>/etc/codex/skills/solve-math-rigorously/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: solve-math-rigorously
description: Produce compact but complete solutions to standalone mathematical questions and proof requests. Use when the user asks to solve, prove, derive, analyze, verify, or correct a mathematical problem that is not primarily an incremental lesson about supplied technical documents. Infer the strongest reasonable interpretation without inventing assumptions, establish a minimal setup, use minimal notation, expose every nontrivial step, state and verify external theorem hypotheses, give proof architecture when useful, and silently audit the draft before answering.
---

# Solve Mathematics Rigorously

Produce a complete mathematical answer rather than a staged document lesson, unless the user explicitly requests incremental pacing. Optimize simultaneously for correctness, rigor, clarity, and economy.

## Determine the intended problem

Interpret the strongest reasonable version supported by the wording and mathematical context. Do not exploit an omission to answer a trivial or uninteresting straw-man version.

Handle ambiguity according to its effect:

- If it does not change the substance, adopt the conventional interpretation and state it briefly.
- If one reasonable assumption makes the intended problem precise, state that assumption before solving.
- If several reasonable interpretations lead to materially different answers, ask for clarification or give a compact case split when that fully resolves the ambiguity.
- If a missing assumption is necessary for the claimed conclusion, do not invent it silently.

If the stated claim is false, say so and give a decisive counterexample or contradiction. Present a corrected nearby statement only when it helps, and label it as a correction rather than the original result.

Answer the problem actually asked. Do not weaken a universal claim to a convenient special case, replace an exact question with a numerical approximation, or prove a converse instead of the stated direction.

## Establish a minimal setup

Begin with a compact setup or preliminaries passage when it reduces ambiguity or supports later reasoning. State only the necessary:

- domain and assumptions;
- definitions used in the solution;
- boundary, regularity, integrability, independence, or nondegeneracy conditions that matter;
- notation that must persist through the answer.

Reuse the problem's notation whenever possible. Introduce a new symbol only when the unabbreviated expression is genuinely cumbersome and recurs often. Define every new symbol before use and state its mathematical type when that is not immediately evident.

Do not force a visible setup heading for a short elementary problem. Do not hide substantive assumptions in prose later in the solution.

## Give the architecture before a substantial proof

Before a long or conceptually difficult proof, explain the plan in a short paragraph or compact list. Identify the main steps, cases, claims, or lemmas and how they combine to yield the conclusion.

Keep the architecture at the level needed to orient the reader. Do not duplicate the detailed proof, and do not substitute a plan for the proof itself. For a routine calculation or one-step argument, omit the architecture.

## Execute every nontrivial step

Derive each nontrivial algebraic, analytic, probabilistic, geometric, combinatorial, or logical transition. Give enough detail for the user to verify the step without reconstructing missing reasoning.

In particular:

- identify the definition, identity, inequality, or prior claim being used;
- show substitutions, rearrangements, case transitions, and changes of variables that carry real mathematical content;
- track domains, signs, quantifiers, equality conditions, exceptional cases, and possible zero denominators;
- justify limit exchanges, differentiation or integration under a sign, convergence claims, and optimization steps;
- distinguish exact arguments from heuristics, approximations, and numerical evidence;
- show that intermediate claims actually imply the requested conclusion.

Avoid words such as “clearly,” “obviously,” “standard,” and “it follows” when they replace reasoning. They are acceptable only after the supporting reason has been supplied.

Do not over-explain elementary arithmetic or definitions already established. Compress routine steps while preserving the chain of verification.

## Invoke external results completely

Whenever the solution relies on a nontrivial theorem or external fact:

1. state the exact form needed, including its assumptions and conclusion;
2. verify each assumption in the present problem;
3. identify the current objects substituted into the theorem;
4. state exactly what conclusion the theorem yields here.

Do not merely name a theorem or call a fact standard. Prefer the relevant specialization over an unnecessarily general statement.

Verify a recent, specialized, source-specific, or uncertain result against a primary or authoritative source before relying on it. Cite the source near the claim. Paraphrase the relevant result unless its exact wording matters; quote only a short compliant excerpt when useful.

If the required theorem is unavailable, uncertain, or does not apply, expose the gap instead of proceeding as though it were valid.

## Use examples and checks proportionately

Include an example, boundary case, counterexample, or remark only when it clarifies an abstraction, tests a hypothesis, explains a consequence, or exposes why an assumption is needed.

Use low-cost sanity checks when they add confidence, such as:

- substituting the result back into the defining relation;
- checking dimensions, signs, or units;
- testing a boundary or small case;
- comparing with a known special case;
- deriving the same conclusion by a second short route.

Do not add examples mechanically or let them replace the general argument.

## Keep the presentation compact

Use headings only when they make a substantial solution easier to navigate. Keep notation sparse and stable. Prefer explicit expressions to disposable shorthand.

Use equations to carry mathematical content, not decoration. Avoid formula dumps, decorative summaries, and boxed equations. State the conclusion plainly.

Let the problem's complexity determine the answer length. Preserve every necessary assumption, theorem check, and logical transition, but remove repetition and exposition that does not improve understanding or verification.

## Audit and revise silently

Draft the solution, then review it before sending. Check:

1. Did the solution address the strongest reasonable interpretation rather than a straw man?
2. Were all adopted assumptions stated, and were none invented merely to force the result?
3. Was the setup sufficient but minimal?
4. Was every persistent symbol necessary and defined?
5. Did a substantial proof receive a useful architecture?
6. Can the reader verify every nontrivial transition?
7. Was every external theorem stated in the needed form, with its hypotheses checked and its conclusion applied explicitly?
8. Were recent, specialized, or uncertain results verified from an authoritative source?
9. Were edge cases, equality conditions, domains, and quantifiers handled?
10. Does the final conclusion answer the exact question?
11. Do examples or remarks earn their space?
12. Is the answer as compact as rigor allows, with no boxed equations?

Revise any failing part and repeat the audit until the answer satisfies the checklist as well as possible. Do not mention the drafting or audit process in the final answer.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 9818 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.916Z | assistant | final_answer ===

### Complete \(L=1\) theorem

Let \(M=M_\phi\) and define

\[
c_{\phi,1}=\frac1{16M}.
\]

For \(G\sim N(0,1)\), write

\[
g=\phi(G),\quad p=\phi'(G),\quad q=\phi''(G),\quad r=\phi'''(G),
\]

and

\[
u=\mathbb E p^4,\qquad
m=\mathbb E[gp^2q],\qquad
j=\mathbb E[p^3r],
\]

\[
e=\mathbb E[p^2q^2],\qquad
\ell=\mathbb E[g^2p^2].
\]

Then

\[
J_{\phi,1}=3j+11m+4u+4\ell+12e.
\]

There is an explicit finite Gaussian-integral constant \(B_{\phi,1}<\infty\) such that, for every \(t\ge1\),

\[
\left|
F_{t,1}(2\eta)-F_{2t,1}(\eta)
+\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,1}\eta^3
\right|
\le B_{\phi,1}t^4|\eta|^5,
\qquad
|\eta|\le\frac1{16M_\phi t}.
\]

The constant is

\[
B_{\phi,1}
=
\mathbb E_{A,U}\,
\mathcal C_{M_\phi}(1+|A|+|U|),
\]

where \(\mathcal C_M\) is defined by the terminating recursion (1.6)–(1.17) in the [complete \(L=1\) proof](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/UNIFORM_L1.md:9).

The proof is genuinely width-first. For every finite width, each neuron is an independent copy of

\[
a_{s+1}=a_s+h\phi(u_s),\qquad
u_{s+1}=u_s+ha_s\phi'(u_s),
\]

so the expected finite-width output already equals

\[
F_{N,1}(h)=\mathbb E[a_N\phi(u_N)].
\]

Writing \(E_h=I+hg\), \(C_h=E_{2h}\), and \(B_h=E_h^2\), one has exactly

\[
B_hz-C_hz
=
h^2\int_0^1Dg(z+shg(z))[g(z)]\,ds.
\]

Telescoping \(t\) macro-steps gives

\[
D_{t,1}(h)=h^2\overline Q_t(h).
\]

For \(R=1+|A|+|U|\), all hybrid trajectories satisfy

\[
1+|a_s|+|u_s|<2R
\]

on \(|h|t\le(16M)^{-1}\), while the derivatives of the vector field and observable through order four are bounded by \(12MR\). The explicit jet recursion consequently proves

\[
\sup_{|h|\le(16Mt)^{-1}}
|\overline Q_t'''(h)|
\le 6B_{\phi,1}t^4.
\]

Its envelope is a polynomial in \(R\) times \(e^{CR}\), hence Gaussian-integrable. This justifies all three differentiations under expectation.

Parity makes \(\overline Q_t\) odd. Taylor’s integral formula therefore yields

\[
|D_{t,1}(h)-\overline Q_t'(0)h^3|
\le B_{\phi,1}t^4|h|^5.
\]

A direct Gaussian calculation gives

\[
\overline Q_t'(0)
=-\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,1}.
\]

The [independent \(L=1\) audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/AUDIT_UNIFORM_L1.md:1) gives a full PASS.

The power \(t^4\) is sharp: for \(\phi(x)=x\),

\[
F_{N,1}(h)
=
\frac{(1+h)^{2N}-(1-h)^{2N}}2,
\]

and the fifth coefficient of the discrepancy is

\[
32{2t\choose5}-{4t\choose5}
=
-\frac43t(t-1)(2t-1)(8t-9),
\]

a degree-four polynomial.

### Why \(L=2\) remains open

The first reused connector appears at \(L=2\). In a fixed chronological representation, strictly historical responses have the causal factorization

\[
\rho_{si}=\varepsilon_i\bar\rho_{si},
\qquad
\sigma_{si}=\varepsilon_i\bar\sigma_{si},
\qquad i<s.
\]

Thus every past response has the correct Volterra step weight. There is also a horizon-independent \(L^2\) energy estimate:

\[
\sum_s|\varepsilon_s|
\le \frac1{216M_\phi^2}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\sup_s
\bigl(
\|a_s\|_2+\|K_s\|_{\mathrm{HS}}+\|u_s\|_2
\bigr)
\le3.
\]

But the first source-tangent equations already contain

\[
a_s\phi''(z_s)\zeta_s^i,
\qquad
r_s\phi''(u_s)p_s^i.
\]

For example,

\[
\|a_s\zeta_s^i\|_2
\le \|a_s\|_4\|\zeta_s^i\|_4,
\]

and the energy estimate controls neither factor. Iterating ordinary Hölder estimates generates an unclosed moment tower. Furthermore, common-step differentiation produces mixed source/generated jets and moving raw actions

\[
I(\partial_hx_s),\qquad J(\partial_h\delta_s),
\]

which are not controlled by the old-source recursions alone.

Consequently no activation-defined \(B_{\phi,2}\) has been constructed. The full derivation is in [UNIFORM_L2.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/UNIFORM_L2.md:1).

### Depth three and general depth

At \(L=3\), the same unresolved response block occurs at both \(W_2\) and \(W_3\). Adding the third layer does not repair the missing one-connector estimate, so no rigorous \(B_{\phi,3}\) is currently available.

The low-depth calculation does reveal the correct prospective general-depth induction:

1. Historical responses carry source-time step weights at every connector.
2. One needs an intrinsic causal Gaussian–Sobolev transfer lemma controlling value, step, source, interpolation, and transported-direction jets.
3. Such a lemma would define an explicit depth recursion
   \[
   \mathfrak R_a=\Gamma_{\phi,a}(\mathfrak R_{a-1}),
   \]
   followed by the already-proved transported-defect argument.
4. Its base case is precisely the still-unresolved \(L=2\) mixed-response estimate.

The independent [combined adversarial audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/AUDIT_UNIFORM_L23.md:1) therefore concludes: \(L=1\) proved; \(L=2\), \(L=3\), and general depth remain open.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 10923 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.953Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes—with an important boundary. The exact requirement for a scalar continuous-time limit is weaker than \(O(t^4)\), and the sharp \(t^4\eta^5\) pressure test can be proved unconditionally for the identity activation at \(L=2\), \(L=3\), and every fixed depth. The corresponding nonlinear OMFP estimate remains open.

For

\[
\Delta_t(\eta)=F_{2t}(\eta)-F_t(2\eta),
\qquad
b_t^*(\rho)=
\sup_{0<|\eta|\le \rho/t}
\frac{|\Delta_t(\eta)-\kappa_t\eta^3|}{|\eta|^5},
\]

the minimal direct scalar condition is

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}
\frac{b_{2^j}^*(\rho)}{2^{5j}}<\infty .
}
\]

Indeed, for \(G_t(T)=F_t(T/t)\),

\[
\|G_{2t}-G_t\|_{\infty,[-R,R]}
\le
\frac{K_\phi R^3}{8t}
+
\frac{b_t^*(\rho)R^5}{32t^5}.
\]

Thus any

\[
b_t^*(\rho)\le C_\phi t^{5-\delta},
\qquad \delta>0,
\]

suffices. Even a bound of order

\[
b_{2^j}^*(\rho)
\lesssim
\frac{2^{5j}}{(1+j)^{1+\delta}}
\]

is enough. Bare \(o(t^5)\) is not sufficient.

For a full state/IDE limit, the better condition is a summable state-level split defect. A particularly useful depth-two hypothesis is the reachable-tail estimate

\[
\sup_{\pi,k}\sup_{p\ge2}
p^{-\alpha}\|Q_k^\pi\|_p
\le K_T,
\qquad
\frac12\le\alpha\le1,
\qquad Q=G^*B.
\]

I proved that this implies the Osgood estimate

\[
\|S_h^2U-S_{2h}U\|
\le
C_T h^2\log^\alpha(e/h),
\]

whose propagated dyadic errors are summable. Consequently the actual width-first discrete states converge to a unique restartable autonomous IDE. This is substantially weaker than controlling fifth derivatives of the terminal output.

The unconditional pressure test closes for \(\phi(x)=x\). Define

\[
K_L=
\max\left\{
1,\,
\max_{0\le r\le4}(L+1)(L)_r3^{L-r},\,
\max_{1\le r\le4}(L+1)_r3^{L+1-r}
\right\},
\qquad
c_L=\frac1{16K_L}.
\]

There is a completely explicit terminating numerical recursion \(C(K)\), recorded in the study, such that

\[
\boxed{
\left|
F_{2t,L}(\eta)-F_{t,L}(2\eta)
-
\frac{t(2t-1)L(L+1)^2(L+2)}3\,\eta^3
\right|
\le
C(K_L)t^4|\eta|^5
}
\]

whenever

\[
|\eta|\le \frac{c_L}{t}.
\]

In particular,

\[
\left|
F_{2t,2}(\eta)-F_{t,2}(2\eta)
-24t(2t-1)\eta^3
\right|
\le C(27)t^4|\eta|^5,
\qquad
|\eta|\le\frac1{432t},
\]

and

\[
\left|
F_{2t,3}(\eta)-F_{t,3}(2\eta)
-80t(2t-1)\eta^3
\right|
\le C(108)t^4|\eta|^5,
\qquad
|\eta|\le\frac1{1728t}.
\]

This proof takes width first at every fixed \((t,\eta)\), retains the same reused \(W,W^*\), and then applies the transported-defect theorem to the resulting trace-class Banach state. The assembled proof passed an independent re-audit.

For genuinely nonlinear activations, the sharp remaining target is an intrinsic response-energy estimate

\[
\mathfrak E_t(\eta)\le C_\phi t^\alpha,
\qquad \alpha<1,
\]

which would imply

\[
b_t^*(\rho_\phi)
\le
\frac{C_\phi}{6}t^{4+\alpha}
\]

and hence dyadic convergence. This is currently unproved at \(L=2\); \(L=3\) additionally needs a connector transfer preserving \(\alpha<1\). The obstruction is the coupled moving-query/aggregate-adjoint response block, not the \(t\)- or \(L\)-combinatorics.

The complete result and audits are here:

- [Main theorem and audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_omfp_continuous_time/THEOREM_AND_AUDIT.md)
- [Unconditional identity theorem](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_omfp_continuous_time/route_d.md)
- [Osgood state-sewing route](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_omfp_continuous_time/route_b.md)
- [Nonlinear response-energy audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_omfp_continuous_time/route_c.md)
- [Evidence ledger](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_omfp_continuous_time/EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md)

<skill>
<name>investigate-conjectures</name>
<path>/etc/codex/skills/investigate-conjectures/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: investigate-conjectures
description: Develop, refine, and adversarially test open mathematical, computational, or mechanistic conjectures through bounded theory–experiment and multi-route proof-search programs. Use when Codex must formulate a non-vacuous research conjecture, recover or update a long-running research program, orchestrate an explicitly authorized search across independent proof approaches, separate proof obligations and claim levels, audit loopholes or competing explanations, design a mechanism-preserving discriminating experiment, interpret new evidence, or synthesize a supersession-aware research state. Do not use for teaching a technical source, solving an isolated mathematical problem, reviewing an external paper, or summarizing material that needs no research-state reconciliation.
---

# Investigate Conjectures

## Purpose

Turn a broad research question into a precise, falsifiable conjecture and an efficient program for resolving its weakest links. Integrate theory, adversarial reasoning, and only authorized computation. Preserve uncertainty: an honest open or inconclusive result is preferable to a stronger unsupported claim.

Treat the skill as the research-program controller. Use a specialized proof, teaching, paper-review, data, or coding skill for bounded subtasks when appropriate, while retaining the claim structure and evidence updates here.

## Load references selectively

- Read [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md) when starting, materially reformulating, or checking the non-vacuity of a conjecture.
- Read [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md) when resuming prior work, reconciling contradictory artifacts, updating conclusions, or writing a master report.
- Read [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) before declaring a loophole closed, a route impossible, a result decisive, or a theorem nearly established.
- Read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) only when computation is explicitly authorized or when the user asks for an experimental design. Do not run an experiment merely because it would be informative.
- Read [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md) when the user requests a bounded multi-route search for a complete mathematical proof. Use parallel agents only when the user explicitly authorizes delegation or parallel work; otherwise apply the same registry and round discipline sequentially.

## Establish scope and authority

1. Restate the research target in one sentence.
2. Determine whether the user authorized:
   - read-only synthesis or assessment;
   - new theoretical analysis;
   - experiment design only;
   - implementation or computation;
   - delegated or parallel proof search;
   - a bounded continuation under conditional branches.
3. Record any time, compute, token, data, model, or run budget as a hard constraint.
4. Ask only for a missing choice that would materially change the result. Otherwise make the narrowest reasonable assumption and label it.
5. Never expand a diagnostic or reporting request into implementation, computation, external actions, or a new research branch.

## Core workflow

### 1. Restore the authoritative state

Read the current primary artifacts before reasoning from memory. Identify the canonical statement, latest valid methods, raw evidence, unresolved objections, and superseded conclusions. Prefer derivations and raw results over summaries; prefer audited later results only when they actually repair or invalidate earlier work.

When resuming or synthesizing, maintain the ledger in [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md).

### 2. Freeze the research contract

Specify the canonical object, admissible inputs, observables, error criterion, topology or norm, horizon, limit order, approximation family, coefficient provenance, complexity dependence, and forbidden substitutions. State what information the proposed surrogate may retain and whether it must be autonomous and restartable.

Apply the non-vacuity checks in [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md). Reject oracle playback, hidden encodings of the target trajectory, replacement by an easier model, or quantifiers that avoid the intended compression problem.

### 3. Build the claim ladder

Separate at least:

1. exact identities or finite constructions;
2. well-posedness or internal correctness of the proposed approximation;
3. empirical accuracy on tested instances;
4. convergence of the approximation hierarchy;
5. identification with the intended limit or real system;
6. compact-horizon validity;
7. all-time or uniform validity;
8. existence of some admissible witness versus success of the current witness.

Do not promote evidence from one rung to another without an explicit bridge.

### 4. State the sharp conjecture

Write explicit quantifiers and dependencies. State:

- the admissible problem class;
- what may depend on accuracy, horizon, and regularity;
- what must remain independent of ambient scale or inaccessible trajectory information;
- the approximation guarantee and norm;
- coefficient provenance and initialization;
- autonomy, restartability, or finite-memory requirements;
- concrete falsifiers.

If several statements are needed, identify one central conjecture and label the rest as lemmas, assumptions, or stronger extensions.

### 5. Derive the exact causal skeleton

Derive before truncating. Mark each step as exact, conditional on named assumptions, heuristic, empirical, or open. Track causal dependencies, information flow, history dependence, and the source of every residual.

Keep distinct approximation axes distinct. Convergence in time step, basis degree, perturbation order, response depth, width, spatial resolution, or another coordinate does not imply convergence in any other axis.

In particular, separate:

- correctness of every fixed-order coefficient from convergence of the full expansion;
- production of omitted error from propagation or stability of that error;
- observable accuracy from state-space convergence;
- failure of a proof route from falsity of the conjecture.

### 6. Build a typed evidence ledger

For each central claim, record its exact statement, claim-ladder rung, status, assumptions, supporting and contrary evidence, dependencies, cheapest resolver, falsifier, and supersession relation.

Use calibrated labels. Distinguish proved, exact under assumptions, empirically supported, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded. Never translate “not observed” into “ruled out.”

### 7. Run a hostile audit

Attack the strongest plausible version of each alternative, not a straw man. Check for:

- hidden memory or inaccessible information;
- tail escape and high-to-low feedback;
- topology or observable mismatch;
- nonnormal amplification and unstable error propagation;
- unjustified interchange of limits;
- easier-model substitution;
- nuisance explanations matched only by weak controls;
- numerical conditioning, discretization, leakage, or selection effects;
- a result that validates one witness but not the intended existence claim.

Use [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) and state what each surviving objection would change.

### 8. Select one bottleneck

Rank candidate next steps by logical leverage, expected uncertainty reduction, mechanism relevance, and cost. Prefer the smallest step whose possible outcomes would change the research state.

Do not optimize for volume of algebra, number of experiments, or apparent completeness. Avoid polishing downstream machinery while an upstream identification or compactness gap remains decisive.

Normally pursue one bottleneck at a time. In an authorized multi-route proof search, retain a small structurally diverse portfolio and select one explicit bottleneck per active route using [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md).

### 9. Precommit any authorized experiment

Before implementation or execution, read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) and freeze:

- competing hypotheses;
- the disputed mechanism and why the testbed preserves it;
- primary metric and matched controls;
- pass, fail, and inconclusive thresholds;
- numerical validity gates;
- replication rule and conditional branches;
- exact budget and terminal stopping condition.

Use the smallest nondegenerate testbed, not automatically the smallest instance. Do not launch exploratory grids when one theory-selected stress or ablation can discriminate the hypotheses.

### 10. Update causally

After a derivation or experiment, ask:

1. What mechanism was supported, rejected, or left unresolved?
2. Which exact ledger claims change status?
3. Does a failure kill the broad conjecture, the current witness, one assumption, or only one proof route?
4. Which earlier conclusion is superseded?
5. Does the result authorize a precommitted branch? If not, stop.

Salvage valid identities, tools, counterexamples, and mechanisms from failed programs. Do not preserve their invalid conclusion.

### 11. Synthesize the current state

Lead with the strongest defensible conclusion. Report separately:

- established results;
- supported but unproved claims;
- disfavored or falsified explanations;
- unresolved decisive gaps;
- superseded conclusions;
- the single highest-leverage next action, if requested and authorized.

When later work corrects earlier work, use the corrected result in the main narrative and record the supersession once. Do not accumulate mutually inconsistent report layers.

## Decision rules

- **Strong surrogate performance:** Treat it as evidence of utility, not hierarchy convergence or arbitrary-accuracy existence.
- **A successful matched nonlinear or causal control:** Downgrade that particular mundane explanation; do not infer a theorem.
- **A failed high-order trend:** Check parity, conditioning, common-reference comparability, and replication before inferring divergence.
- **A correct local expansion with growing coefficients:** Preserve the local algebra while rejecting unsupported positive-horizon convergence.
- **A stability theorem without a source estimate:** Record propagation control; leave error production open.
- **Failure of one representation:** Distinguish failure of the explicit witness from failure of the broader admissible class.
- **Confounded evidence:** Mark inconclusive and stop unless a resolution run was preauthorized.
- **Budget exhausted:** Stop even when the scientific question remains open.

## Output standards

Match the requested artifact. For a concise assessment, provide the current conclusion, decisive evidence, and leading gap. For a full research state, provide:

1. canonical target and research contract;
2. sharp conjecture;
3. exact causal skeleton;
4. claim and evidence ledger;
5. adversarial alternatives;
6. experimental evidence and validity limits;
7. what is established, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded;
8. ranked proof obligations or next decision.

Keep notation stable and minimal. Attach claims to evidence close to where they appear. State assumptions at the claim they support. Never hide a decisive gap in a generic limitations section.

</skill>

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 11196 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.962Z | assistant | commentary ===
The idea has a real mechanism, but not through ordinary continuity. For every nonzero perturbation amplitude, the ambient \(L^2\) second-derivative norm jumps from finite to infinite, so the identity Banach theorem is not open in that topology. The promising reformulation is coefficientwise: expand the exact causal response ledger in the activation amplitude. At order zero the response system is the solved linear Volterra system; at order \(k\), the highest unknown should see only that same resolvent, while genuinely nonlinear products use lower amplitude orders. If this triangularity survives the mixed moving-query jets, it gives an amplitude radius independent of \(t\). That is the bottleneck the independent routes are now testing.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 12000 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.984Z | assistant | final_answer ===
The idea was only partially tried before: a normalized residual-sine activation was considered, but not this coefficientwise, horizon-uniform homotopy argument.

After developing and independently auditing it, the verdict is:

- It is a genuinely promising OMFP direction.
- It proves a nontrivial nonlinear openness theorem for the cubic coefficient at \(L=2\) and \(L=3\).
- It does not yet prove the required uniform \(t^4|h|^5\) remainder for any fixed nonzero nonlinear interval. That remains open already at \(L=2\).

For

\[
\psi_{\alpha,\varphi}(x)
=
\frac{x+\alpha\varphi(x)}
{\|G+\alpha\varphi(G)\|_2},
\qquad
\max_{0\le r\le12}\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le R,
\]

I proved

\[
\left|
J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}-J_{\mathrm{id},L}
\right|
\le \mathcal L_L|\alpha|R,
\]

where \(\mathcal L_L\) is given by an explicit terminating recursion involving nine Gaussian activation moments, and

\[
J_{\mathrm{id},L}
=
\frac{2L(L+1)^2(L+2)}{3}.
\]

Thus \(J_{\mathrm{id},2}=48\) and \(J_{\mathrm{id},3}=160\). Coarse fully numerical sufficient nonlinear intervals are

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{24}{85475R}
\quad (L=2),
\]

and

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{80}{8610421R}
\quad (L=3).
\]

On these intervals,

\[
J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}
\ge \frac12J_{\mathrm{id},L}>0.
\]

This is a genuine nonlinear theorem, but only for the cubic coefficient

\[
\kappa_{\alpha,L,t}
=
\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}
\]

in \(F_{2t,L}(h)-F_{t,L}(2h)\).

The favorable new mechanism is exact coefficientwise triangularity. At every fixed finite schedule and finite marked expression, amplitude order \(k\) satisfies schematically

\[
\mathcal L^{\langle k\rangle}
=
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{id}}\,
\mathcal S_k
\bigl(
\mathcal L^{\langle0\rangle},
\ldots,
\mathcal L^{\langle k-1\rangle}
\bigr).
\]

Hence the highest amplitude order is acted on only by the identity-background linear response operator; genuine nonlinear terms involve lower amplitude orders. This is precisely why the idea has a good shot.

However, ordinary continuity is insufficient:

1. The \(C^2(L^2,L^2)\) Nemytskii seminorm is infinite for every genuinely nonlinear \(\alpha\ne0\), although it vanishes at \(\alpha=0\).

2. Fixed-\(t\) analyticity cannot yield a radius uniform in \(t\); for example, \(t^4e^{\alpha^2t^2}\) has all the pointwise properties but no fixed useful nonlinear interval.

3. Most decisively, reused adjoints generate an unbounded source-derivative hierarchy:

\[
J^*V=\mathbb E[D_JV],
\qquad
J^*(D_\zeta V)=\mathbb E[D_JD_\zeta V].
\]

Thus a one-source ledger creates a two-source ledger, and repeated reuse creates every source order. Two independent audits caught that a proposed Volterra closure had silently hidden this hierarchy inside its Gronwall constant.

The first unresolved generated susceptibility is already

\[
\frac12\varphi''(z^{\langle0\rangle})
\bigl(J^*x^{\langle1\rangle}\bigr)^2,
\]

including its three normalized mesh derivatives, transported-defect mark, and all aggregate source descendants.

So the correct next theorem is an all-source analytic-scale/factorial-contraction estimate combining:

- chronological simplex factorials;
- Gaussian/Wick contraction gains;
- aggregate grouping before norms at singular Grams.

That is a concrete sufficient research program, but it is not proved. Consequently, the uniform nonlinear remainder and continuous-time conclusion remain open at \(L=2\), and therefore also at \(L=3\). No counterexample to the desired theorem was found.

The complete audited record is in:

- [AUDITED_SYNTHESIS.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/AUDITED_SYNTHESIS.md)
- [CUBIC_OPENNESS.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/CUBIC_OPENNESS.md)
- [EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md)
- [FOURIER_SUBCLASS_SUPPLEMENT.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/FOURIER_SUBCLASS_SUPPLEMENT.md)

<skill>
<name>investigate-conjectures</name>
<path>/etc/codex/skills/investigate-conjectures/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: investigate-conjectures
description: Develop, refine, and adversarially test open mathematical, computational, or mechanistic conjectures through bounded theory–experiment and multi-route proof-search programs. Use when Codex must formulate a non-vacuous research conjecture, recover or update a long-running research program, orchestrate an explicitly authorized search across independent proof approaches, separate proof obligations and claim levels, audit loopholes or competing explanations, design a mechanism-preserving discriminating experiment, interpret new evidence, or synthesize a supersession-aware research state. Do not use for teaching a technical source, solving an isolated mathematical problem, reviewing an external paper, or summarizing material that needs no research-state reconciliation.
---

# Investigate Conjectures

## Purpose

Turn a broad research question into a precise, falsifiable conjecture and an efficient program for resolving its weakest links. Integrate theory, adversarial reasoning, and only authorized computation. Preserve uncertainty: an honest open or inconclusive result is preferable to a stronger unsupported claim.

Treat the skill as the research-program controller. Use a specialized proof, teaching, paper-review, data, or coding skill for bounded subtasks when appropriate, while retaining the claim structure and evidence updates here.

## Load references selectively

- Read [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md) when starting, materially reformulating, or checking the non-vacuity of a conjecture.
- Read [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md) when resuming prior work, reconciling contradictory artifacts, updating conclusions, or writing a master report.
- Read [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) before declaring a loophole closed, a route impossible, a result decisive, or a theorem nearly established.
- Read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) only when computation is explicitly authorized or when the user asks for an experimental design. Do not run an experiment merely because it would be informative.
- Read [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md) when the user requests a bounded multi-route search for a complete mathematical proof. Use parallel agents only when the user explicitly authorizes delegation or parallel work; otherwise apply the same registry and round discipline sequentially.

## Establish scope and authority

1. Restate the research target in one sentence.
2. Determine whether the user authorized:
   - read-only synthesis or assessment;
   - new theoretical analysis;
   - experiment design only;
   - implementation or computation;
   - delegated or parallel proof search;
   - a bounded continuation under conditional branches.
3. Record any time, compute, token, data, model, or run budget as a hard constraint.
4. Ask only for a missing choice that would materially change the result. Otherwise make the narrowest reasonable assumption and label it.
5. Never expand a diagnostic or reporting request into implementation, computation, external actions, or a new research branch.

## Core workflow

### 1. Restore the authoritative state

Read the current primary artifacts before reasoning from memory. Identify the canonical statement, latest valid methods, raw evidence, unresolved objections, and superseded conclusions. Prefer derivations and raw results over summaries; prefer audited later results only when they actually repair or invalidate earlier work.

When resuming or synthesizing, maintain the ledger in [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md).

### 2. Freeze the research contract

Specify the canonical object, admissible inputs, observables, error criterion, topology or norm, horizon, limit order, approximation family, coefficient provenance, complexity dependence, and forbidden substitutions. State what information the proposed surrogate may retain and whether it must be autonomous and restartable.

Apply the non-vacuity checks in [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md). Reject oracle playback, hidden encodings of the target trajectory, replacement by an easier model, or quantifiers that avoid the intended compression problem.

### 3. Build the claim ladder

Separate at least:

1. exact identities or finite constructions;
2. well-posedness or internal correctness of the proposed approximation;
3. empirical accuracy on tested instances;
4. convergence of the approximation hierarchy;
5. identification with the intended limit or real system;
6. compact-horizon validity;
7. all-time or uniform validity;
8. existence of some admissible witness versus success of the current witness.

Do not promote evidence from one rung to another without an explicit bridge.

### 4. State the sharp conjecture

Write explicit quantifiers and dependencies. State:

- the admissible problem class;
- what may depend on accuracy, horizon, and regularity;
- what must remain independent of ambient scale or inaccessible trajectory information;
- the approximation guarantee and norm;
- coefficient provenance and initialization;
- autonomy, restartability, or finite-memory requirements;
- concrete falsifiers.

If several statements are needed, identify one central conjecture and label the rest as lemmas, assumptions, or stronger extensions.

### 5. Derive the exact causal skeleton

Derive before truncating. Mark each step as exact, conditional on named assumptions, heuristic, empirical, or open. Track causal dependencies, information flow, history dependence, and the source of every residual.

Keep distinct approximation axes distinct. Convergence in time step, basis degree, perturbation order, response depth, width, spatial resolution, or another coordinate does not imply convergence in any other axis.

In particular, separate:

- correctness of every fixed-order coefficient from convergence of the full expansion;
- production of omitted error from propagation or stability of that error;
- observable accuracy from state-space convergence;
- failure of a proof route from falsity of the conjecture.

### 6. Build a typed evidence ledger

For each central claim, record its exact statement, claim-ladder rung, status, assumptions, supporting and contrary evidence, dependencies, cheapest resolver, falsifier, and supersession relation.

Use calibrated labels. Distinguish proved, exact under assumptions, empirically supported, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded. Never translate “not observed” into “ruled out.”

### 7. Run a hostile audit

Attack the strongest plausible version of each alternative, not a straw man. Check for:

- hidden memory or inaccessible information;
- tail escape and high-to-low feedback;
- topology or observable mismatch;
- nonnormal amplification and unstable error propagation;
- unjustified interchange of limits;
- easier-model substitution;
- nuisance explanations matched only by weak controls;
- numerical conditioning, discretization, leakage, or selection effects;
- a result that validates one witness but not the intended existence claim.

Use [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) and state what each surviving objection would change.

### 8. Select one bottleneck

Rank candidate next steps by logical leverage, expected uncertainty reduction, mechanism relevance, and cost. Prefer the smallest step whose possible outcomes would change the research state.

Do not optimize for volume of algebra, number of experiments, or apparent completeness. Avoid polishing downstream machinery while an upstream identification or compactness gap remains decisive.

Normally pursue one bottleneck at a time. In an authorized multi-route proof search, retain a small structurally diverse portfolio and select one explicit bottleneck per active route using [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md).

### 9. Precommit any authorized experiment

Before implementation or execution, read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) and freeze:

- competing hypotheses;
- the disputed mechanism and why the testbed preserves it;
- primary metric and matched controls;
- pass, fail, and inconclusive thresholds;
- numerical validity gates;
- replication rule and conditional branches;
- exact budget and terminal stopping condition.

Use the smallest nondegenerate testbed, not automatically the smallest instance. Do not launch exploratory grids when one theory-selected stress or ablation can discriminate the hypotheses.

### 10. Update causally

After a derivation or experiment, ask:

1. What mechanism was supported, rejected, or left unresolved?
2. Which exact ledger claims change status?
3. Does a failure kill the broad conjecture, the current witness, one assumption, or only one proof route?
4. Which earlier conclusion is superseded?
5. Does the result authorize a precommitted branch? If not, stop.

Salvage valid identities, tools, counterexamples, and mechanisms from failed programs. Do not preserve their invalid conclusion.

### 11. Synthesize the current state

Lead with the strongest defensible conclusion. Report separately:

- established results;
- supported but unproved claims;
- disfavored or falsified explanations;
- unresolved decisive gaps;
- superseded conclusions;
- the single highest-leverage next action, if requested and authorized.

When later work corrects earlier work, use the corrected result in the main narrative and record the supersession once. Do not accumulate mutually inconsistent report layers.

## Decision rules

- **Strong surrogate performance:** Treat it as evidence of utility, not hierarchy convergence or arbitrary-accuracy existence.
- **A successful matched nonlinear or causal control:** Downgrade that particular mundane explanation; do not infer a theorem.
- **A failed high-order trend:** Check parity, conditioning, common-reference comparability, and replication before inferring divergence.
- **A correct local expansion with growing coefficients:** Preserve the local algebra while rejecting unsupported positive-horizon convergence.
- **A stability theorem without a source estimate:** Record propagation control; leave error production open.
- **Failure of one representation:** Distinguish failure of the explicit witness from failure of the broader admissible class.
- **Confounded evidence:** Mark inconclusive and stop unless a resolution run was preauthorized.
- **Budget exhausted:** Stop even when the scientific question remains open.

## Output standards

Match the requested artifact. For a concise assessment, provide the current conclusion, decisive evidence, and leading gap. For a full research state, provide:

1. canonical target and research contract;
2. sharp conjecture;
3. exact causal skeleton;
4. claim and evidence ledger;
5. adversarial alternatives;
6. experimental evidence and validity limits;
7. what is established, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded;
8. ranked proof obligations or next decision.

Keep notation stable and minimal. Attach claims to evidence close to where they appear. State assumptions at the claim they support. Never hide a decisive gap in a generic limitations section.

</skill>

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 12072 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.986Z | assistant | final_answer ===
The idea is promising, but the proposed continuity argument is not sufficient to prove the nonlinear \(L=2\) remainder theorem. After several proof routes and independent hostile audits, that theorem remains open.

It had been tried partially: a normalized residual-sine activation had previously been identified as a good near-identity candidate. The horizon-uniform, amplitude-triangular OMFP formulation you propose had not been completed.

### What is now proved

For

\[
\psi_{\alpha,\varphi}(x)
=
\frac{x+\alpha\varphi(x)}
{\|G+\alpha\varphi(G)\|_2},
\qquad
\max_{r\le12}\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le R,
\]

the exact Gaussian cubic invariant is Lipschitz:

\[
\left|
J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}-J_{\mathrm{id},L}
\right|
\le \mathcal L_L |\alpha|R,
\]

where \(\mathcal L_L\) is given by a finite activation-moment recursion. Since

\[
J_{\mathrm{id},2}=48,
\qquad
J_{\mathrm{id},3}=160,
\]

we obtain genuine nonlinear intervals on which the cubic coefficient remains nonzero. Explicit sufficient choices are

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{24}{85475R}
\quad (L=2),
\]

and

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{80}{8610421R}
\quad (L=3).
\]

These statements concern the actual width-first OMFP cubic coefficient, not an abstract polynomial. They do not yet control the fifth-order remainder.

A natural purely activation-defined nonlinear energy is

\[
\mathcal E_\rho(\psi)
=
\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}
\frac{\rho^r}{r!}\|\psi^{(r)}\|_\infty.
\]

For the Bernstein class

\[
\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le MA^r,
\]

one has explicitly

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E_\rho(\psi_{\alpha,\varphi})
\le
\frac43|\alpha|M
\bigl(e^{A\rho}-1-A\rho\bigr).
}
\]

This is, I think, the right local notion of nonlinear energy: it measures precisely the curvature insertions absent at identity.

### Why identity is special

At identity, every derivative of activation order at least two vanishes. Consequently:

- nonlinear response branching disappears;
- the moving-source hierarchy collapses;
- the reused-matrix response is governed by a linear Hilbert/trace-class block;
- the transported-defect argument closes with \(t^4|h|^5\).

Around identity there is an exact favorable triangularity. At every fixed finite schedule and amplitude order \(k\),

\[
\mathcal L^{\langle k\rangle}
=
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{id}}\,
\mathcal S_k
\left(
\mathcal L^{\langle0\rangle},
\ldots,
\mathcal L^{\langle k-1\rangle}
\right).
\]

Thus the order-\(k\) unknown never enters the nonlinear forcing; only the identity-background linear response operator propagates it. This is the strongest evidence that your homotopy has a genuine chance.

### The decisive obstruction

Ordinary \(L^2\) Lipschitz or \(C^2\) perturbation theory cannot work. For every genuinely nonlinear \(\alpha\ne0\), the Nemytskii second-derivative seminorm is infinite, even though it is zero at identity.

More importantly, reused adjoints raise Gaussian source order:

\[
J^*V=\mathbb E[D_JV],
\qquad
J^*(D_\zeta V)=\mathbb E[D_JD_\zeta V].
\]

Applying another adjoint requests another source derivative. Hence any finite source-response ledger fails to close. An attempted \(L=2\) Volterra proof silently assumed the required aggregate response-row estimate at exactly this point; two independent audits rejected it.

The first unresolved concrete estimate is the generated-core bound

\[
\sup_{\substack{t,s\\ |h|t\le\rho}}
\left\|
t^{-q}\partial_h^q\partial_\lambda^r
J^*x_s^{\langle1\rangle}
\right\|_4
<\infty,
\qquad
q\le3,\quad r\le1,
\]

including all its higher source-marked aggregate descendants.

Small \(\mathcal E_\rho(\psi)\) controls every local nonlinear vertex, but no proof currently shows that chronological and Gaussian factorial cancellations control the entire resulting response tree uniformly in \(t\).

### Audited conclusion

So the sharp answer is:

- Your idea is one of the best current routes.
- It unconditionally produces a genuine nonlinear class for the cubic coefficient at \(L=2\) and \(L=3\).
- The right proximity is an analytic curvature energy augmented by an all-source aggregate-response contraction norm.
- A purely activation-local curvature bound is not yet known to imply the required global response bound.
- Therefore the uniform nonlinear \(t^4|h|^5\) theorem remains open already for \(L=2\), and consequently for \(L=3\).

The next theorem must combine chronological simplex factorials, Wick-contraction gains, and intrinsic aggregation at singular reused-matrix Grams. I recorded that as a sufficient conjectural program, not as a proved result.

Full artifacts:

- [Audited synthesis](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/AUDITED_SYNTHESIS.md)
- [Proved cubic openness theorem](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/CUBIC_OPENNESS.md)
- [Adversarial response/Fourier audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/FOURIER_SUBCLASS_SUPPLEMENT.md)
- [Claim and evidence ledger](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_near_identity_omfp/EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md)
